# Farghānī — cumulative graded bilingual selected-locus corpus (Stage XIII, 9 November 2026)

**468 individual selected authorial Arabic–English excerpt records, from B2007 printed-page labels 18–101.** This is the FULL merged record set for passes 001–009, but **NOT a complete Arabic transcription, not full line-by-line page collation, and not a definitive critical edition**. For full continuously page-labelled working English consult [WORKING_TRANSLATION_18_145.md](WORKING_TRANSLATION_18_145.md). For the entire older 1.8MB dossier consult [FULL_CUMULATIVE_CRITICAL_007.md](FULL_CUMULATIVE_CRITICAL_007.md).

**Grade-separated counts:** 283 directly inspected modern B2007 edited-digitized selected phrases (pp18–60, 43 page labels); 185 authorial Arabic phrases read in a **secondary retranscription of that modern edited edition** (pp61–101, 41 page labels). Do NOT regard the secondary copy as independent historical text evidence. Previous 10 bounded photographed **I1876 printing** comparisons remain only 10, with five print differences and five print agreements; zero medieval handwritten manuscript leaf collated, zero MSS variants. Page labels with any selected check = 84/128. Printed pages completely source-proofread = 0.

**Editing method:** Arabic span and English are given at the selected bounded locus; earlier verification disposition and notes retained. Each page heading links the actual source used. A modern-forum source is never a second witness. Page/printed witnesses and conjectures are separately flagged. Exact machine-readable source for each record is listed at the end.

## Beirut B2007 printed page 18 · B2007 modern digitized edited source

Page/source: https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/9606/18?lang=ar

### CR001-001 · revise

**Arabic:** الأصل الأول في ذكر رتب الذات وتعيّن الأسماء منها والصفات وذكر كيفية انتشاء الفروع من تلك الأسماء والصفات من أصولها مع تعرّض لشرح الأسماء التسعة والتسعين ، وذكر انتشاء باقي الأسماء منها دقيقها وجليلها

**English:** First foundation: the ranks of the Essence, the determination of names and attributes from it, the derivation of their branches from their roots, an account of the ninety-nine names, and the derivation of the other names, subtle and great.

**Editorial decision:** The older English said 'differentiation' for تعيّن; preserve the technical 'determination'.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-002 · annotate

**Arabic:** بِسْمِ اللَّهِ الرَّحْمنِ الرَّحِيمِ نقل عن رسول اللّه صلّى اللّه عليه وسلّم أنه قال : « قال اللّه عزّ وجلّ : كنت كنزا مخفيّا فأحببت أن أعرف ، فخلقت الخلق لأعرف »

**English:** In the name of God, the All-merciful, the Especially Merciful. It is transmitted as from the Messenger of God that God said: 'I was a hidden treasure, and I loved to be known, so I created creation in order to be known.'

**Editorial decision:** Farghānī reports an attribution; the 'hidden treasure' saying lacks an established prophetic isnād. Its interpretive importance must not be treated as authentication.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-003 · retain

**Arabic:** اعلم أيّدك اللّه بالفهم الصحيح أنه قد كنى في هذا الحديث الإلهي عن كنه الغيب وإطلاق الذات الأقدس وباطن الهوية الأزلية بالكنز المخفي

**English:** Know—may God strengthen you with sound understanding—that the hidden treasure in this divine saying figuratively signifies the innermost reality of the Unseen, the nondelimitation of the most holy Essence, and the inward aspect of eternal Identity.

**Editorial decision:** Keep kunh (inner reality) distinct from its iṭlāq (nondelimitation).

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-004 · revise

**Arabic:** وذلك لملابسة أن الكنز عبارة عن عين مغيب مكنون وسرّ مخزون مشتمل على جواهر عظيمة الجدوى مرغوب فيها

**English:** The analogy arises because a treasure is a concealed and guarded thing, a secret held in reserve, comprising precious jewels whose benefits are great and which are much sought after.

**Editorial decision:** The prior paraphrase omitted the explicit 'great benefit' (ʿaẓīmat al-jadwā) and being sought (marghūb fīhā).

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-005 · retain

**Arabic:** ما بين أنفس تصان ويضنّ ويستأثر بها وبين نفيس ، ربما يقع فيه التسامح والتبدّل خصوصا أو عموما

**English:** Some of these are so precious that they are guarded, withheld and reserved to their possessor; others are precious but may be dealt with more freely or exchanged, privately or generally.

**Editorial decision:** The second comparison is between anfas and nafīs, degrees of relative preciousness.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-006 · retain

**Arabic:** ومهما أريد إظهاره يعرض أولا عرضا مجملا بحيث يبين الكنز ويظهر لواجده في محله

**English:** When it is desired to disclose a treasure, it is first displayed as a whole, so that the treasure becomes evident to its finder in its own place.

**Editorial decision:** Preserve ijmāl (undifferentiated comprehensiveness), not simply a generic presentation.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-007 · retain

**Arabic:** ثم يعرض ثانيا عرضا يميّز فيه بين الأنفس والنفيس ، وينقل منه عن محله لحكمة يراها الواجد من إظهار كماله الذي يتضمّن غناءه وإظهار التجمّل والجود ونحو ذلك

**English:** It is then shown a second time in a display that differentiates what is most precious from what is merely precious; some is removed from its place according to a wisdom perceived by the finder, to display his perfection, including wealth, adornment, generosity and similar qualities.

**Editorial decision:** A figurative sequence of comprehensive and then differentiated disclosure; not a temporal sequence in the divine Essence.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-008 · retain

**Arabic:** وكذلك كنه الجناب الأقدس تعالى وتقدّس وله المثل الأعلى في السماوات والأرض وهو العزيز الحكيم ، بلا نسبة تشبيه وتشكيل وتمثيل إليه تعالى عن ذلك علوّا كبيرا

**English:** Likewise with the innermost reality of the most holy Presence—exalted and sanctified is He, to whom belongs the highest similitude in the heavens and earth, the Mighty and Wise—without attributing to Him any likeness, configuration or representation. He is vastly exalted above that.

**Editorial decision:** The anti-anthropomorphic qualification is part of the authorial analogy, not an editorial gloss.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-009 · retain

**Arabic:** فإنه الغيب المكنون والسرّ المصون الذي هو أبطن كل باطن

**English:** For He is the hidden Unseen and the protected secret, more inward than every inward thing.

**Editorial decision:** This is a divine negative-theological predication in analogy.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-010 · crux

**Arabic:** وبطون المشتمل على جواهر أسماء الذات التي هي أنفس نفائس الأسماء

**English:** His inwardness contains the jewels of the essential names, the most precious of all names.

**Editorial decision:** The digital text has the awkward sequence بطون المشتمل. Translation conveys contextual sense but a print/manuscript reading is needed before fixing grammar.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: true; medieval manuscript verified: false.

**Bounded historical printing comparison:** I1876 printed p5, scan 9: **وبطون المشتمل** (agreement); Both historical printed p5 and digitized Beirut show the difficult wording; leave open for manuscript.. This is not a manuscript variant.

### CR001-011 · retain

**Arabic:** التي منها ما يستأثر في مكنون الغيب عنده ، فلا يعلمها إلّا هو ومن ارتفعت بينونته ورفعت كينونته ممن هو أكمل جميع

**English:** Among them are names He reserves in the hidden mystery with Him; none knows them except He and one whose separateness and individual selfhood have been lifted away, one of the most perfect among all—

**Editorial decision:** The clause continues across B2007 p.18–19; the wording must not be treated as a finished independent proposition.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

## Beirut B2007 printed page 19 · B2007 modern digitized edited source

Page/source: https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/9606/19?lang=ar

### CR001-012 · retain

**Arabic:** الكمّل في عرض التعيّن والتجلّي الأول ، لتحقيق الكمال الذاتي الأشرف المعني بقوله : « فأحببت أن أعرف »

**English:** —the perfected, in the presentation of the first determination and first self-disclosure, for realizing the nobler essential perfection intended by 'I loved to be known'.

**Editorial decision:** Continuation from p18; 'in the presentation' preferred to the slightly ambiguous 'participate in the display'.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-013 · revise

**Arabic:** وهو المتضمّن أيضا نفائس درر أسماء الصفات التي يسمح بتعريفها لكل من يصلح لتشريفها من كل فرد متخلّق ، أو كامل متحقّق

**English:** It also contains the precious jewels of the names of attributes, acquaintance with which is permitted to anyone suited to be honored by them, whether one who has adopted their character or a perfected person who has realized them.

**Editorial decision:** Distinguish takhalluq (assuming qualities) from taḥaqquq (realizing them).

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-014 · corrected

**Arabic:** والمحتوي أيضا على لآلىء أسماء الأفعال العامّ نفعها وأثرها ، والمستفيض حكمها وخبرها في جميع المراتب الكونيّة التي هي محال تمام الكمال الأسمائي والمطلب الغائبّ المعني بقوله : « فخلقت الخلق لأعرف »

**English:** It also contains the pearls of the names of acts, whose benefits and effects are universal, and whose judgments and reports extend throughout the cosmic ranks—the loci in which the perfection through the names is completed, the ultimate objective referred to by 'I created creation to be known'.

**Editorial decision:** **Historical-print correction:** I1876 printed p6 (PDF scan 10) reads المطلب الغائي, while B2007 digital reads المطلب الغائبّ. Prefer ‘ultimate objective’ for the edited text; preserve the B2007 form in apparatus. This is a real print-level difference, not a manuscript variant.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: true; medieval manuscript verified: false.

**Bounded historical printing comparison:** I1876 printed p6, scan 10: **والمطلب الغائي** (historical-print-difference); I1876 p6 visibly reads الغائي where B2007 digital reads الغائبّ. Prefer historic print for a purposive objective; no manuscript inferred.. This is not a manuscript variant.

### CR001-015 · retain

**Arabic:** فقوله : « كنت » يخبر عن تعيّن مسبوق بالإطلاق

**English:** Thus 'I was' tells of a determination preceded by nondelimitation.

**Editorial decision:** Priority of rank is explicated later, not of time.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-016 · revise

**Arabic:** فإن مفهوم تاء المتكلّم عن نفسه يؤذن بتعيّنه في نفسه وظهوره حالة تكلّمه تعيّنا وظهورا

**English:** The first-person tāʾ, by which the speaker speaks of himself, implies his determination in himself and his manifestation when speaking, as determination and manifestation.

**Editorial decision:** More literal account of tāʾ al-mutakallim than the earlier compressed 'first-person marker' paraphrase.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-017 · retain

**Arabic:** أما قوله : « كنزا مخفيّا » ينبئ عن غيبه وإطلاقه بحيث لا يدخل تحت حكم معيّن يحكم عليه من حيث هذا الغيب والإطلاق

**English:** By contrast, 'a hidden treasure' signifies His unseen character and nondelimitation, in which no determinate judgment can be applied to Him precisely insofar as He is unseen and undelimited.

**Editorial decision:** The qualification min ḥayth is crucial; not an unrestricted negation of divine predication.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-018 · retain

**Arabic:** ولا تحت إحاطة به علما من حيث تعيّناته أصلا ، فإنّ مطلق الخفاء يحكم بذلك كلّه

**English:** Nor can He be encompassed by knowledge through His determinations in this respect; absolute hiddenness entails all this.

**Editorial decision:** Scope of the negation is the Essence precisely as hidden and undelimited.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-019 · retain

**Arabic:** وحيث كان الخفاء وقع خبرا « لكنت » عرف سبق الإطلاق والغيب على ذلك التعيّن ، ولكن سبقا رتبيّا لا زمانيّا ، فإنه صحّ أنه ليس عند اللّه صباح ولا مساء

**English:** Since hiddenness is predicated of 'I was', nondelimitation and the Unseen are understood to precede that determination—but by priority of rank, not priority in time; for there is neither morning nor evening with God.

**Editorial decision:** The source explicitly supplies sabqan rutbiyyan lā zamāniyyan.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-020 · retain

**Arabic:** وقوله : « فأحببت » يخبر عن ميل أصلي هو وصلة بين الخفاء والظهور المذكور

**English:** 'Then I loved' tells of an original inclination linking the hiddenness and manifestation just described.

**Editorial decision:** Maḥabba is not a subsequent temporal event.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-021 · retain

**Arabic:** وما ثمّ إلّا التعيّن الذي هو عين الوحدة ، فكان عينه

**English:** There is nothing there except the determination that is identical with unity; the inclination is therefore identical with it.

**Editorial decision:** The return of ʿaynuhu needs to be kept with its referent, not a second existing subject.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-022 · retain

**Arabic:** وقوله : « أن أعرف » يشير إلى ظهور ذلك الخافي

**English:** 'That I might be known' points to the manifestation of what had been hidden.

**Editorial decision:** An interpretive relation to a reported saying.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-023 · retain

**Arabic:** وقوله : « فخلقت الخلق لأعرف » يعرف أن تقدير المقدّرات علما ووجودا يعقب ذلك الميل ويترتّب على متعلّقه الذي هو الظهور الحاصل بذلك التعيّن

**English:** 'So I created creation to be known' indicates that the determining of what is to be determined, in knowledge and in existence, follows that inclination and is ordered to what it seeks: the manifestation arising through that determination.

**Editorial decision:** This clause has علما ووجودا explicitly, distinct from the uncertain reading of عمليا below.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-024 · corrected

**Arabic:** ليتحقّق كمال ذلك الظهور مفصّلا متميّزا ويشمل حكمه وأثره المقدّر والمقدّر تعقّبا ذاتيّا لا زمانيّا ، بل عمليّا ووجوديّا أيضا بالنسبة إلى المقدّر العالم الأزلي وعلمه الذاتي والصفاتي الأبدي

**English:** So that the perfection of disclosure may be realized in articulated distinction, and its rule and effect may encompass the determiner and what is determined in essential rather than temporal succession—and also in knowledge and existence with respect to the eternal Determiner who knows, and His everlasting essential and attributive knowledge.

**Editorial decision:** **Historical-print correction:** I1876 printed p6, PDF scan 10, reads بل علميا ووجوديا; B2007 digital prints بل عمليّا ووجوديّا. Correct the edited reading to ‘in knowledge and existence’, supported by the immediate wording about the eternal knower and His knowledge. No manuscript readings verified.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: true; medieval manuscript verified: false.

**Bounded historical printing comparison:** I1876 printed p6, scan 10: **بل علميا ووجوديا أيضا** (historical-print-difference); I1876 p6 visibly reads علميا where B2007 digital reads عمليا. Retain historical print reading and existing epistemic/existential sense.. This is not a manuscript variant.

### CR001-025 · retain

**Arabic:** فإن علمه عين وجوده ، فافهم تقرّ بالكبريت الأحمر والكنز الأكبر إن شاء اللّه تعالى

**English:** For His knowledge is identical with His existence. Understand, and—God willing—you will rejoice in the red sulphur and the greatest treasure.

**Editorial decision:** The independent clause returns to the main metaphysical assertion.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-026 · retain

**Arabic:** ثم اعلم أن كنه الذات الأقدس وغيب الهوية والإطلاق والأزلية المندرج فيه حكم الأبدية لا يشهد ولا يفهم ولا يعلم ولا يدرك من حيث التعيّن أصلا

**English:** Know further that the innermost reality of the most holy Essence, the hiddenness of Identity, nondelimitation and pre-eternity—in which the determination of everlastingness is included—cannot be witnessed, understood, known or apprehended from the standpoint of any determination whatsoever.

**Editorial decision:** Important: the denial concerns apprehension of the undelimited Essence as such, not all knowledge of God through self-disclosure.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-027 · retain

**Arabic:** ولا يدخل تحت حكم متعيّن البتّة . نعم اللّهمّ إلا حكما سلبيّا بأنها لا تعلم

**English:** Nor can it fall under any determinate judgment at all—except, perhaps, a negative judgment that it is not knowable.

**Editorial decision:** The limiting exception is not an affirmative grasp of the Essence.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-028 · retain

**Arabic:** وإنما توجه وتأتى هذا الحكم السلبي أيضا بأحد وجهين ؛ أحدهما : إخبار إلهي عن ذلك

**English:** Even this negative judgment can be directed or attained only in one of two ways. The first is divine report concerning it—

**Editorial decision:** The sentence continues on p20.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

## Beirut B2007 printed page 20 · B2007 modern digitized edited source

Page/source: https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/9606/20?lang=ar

### CR001-029 · annotate

**Arabic:** بقوله تعالى : وَلا يُحِيطُونَ بِهِ عِلْماً [ طه : الآية 110 ] ، وبقوله : « كنت كنزا مخفيّا » ونحو ذلك

**English:** —as in God's words, 'They do not encompass Him in knowledge' (Qurʾān 20:110), and the saying 'I was a hidden treasure', and similar expressions.

**Editorial decision:** Distinguish an actual Qurʾānic verse from the unattested 'hidden treasure' saying, though Farghānī lists both as divine report.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-030 · retain

**Arabic:** والوجه الثاني : انقطاع أكمل الإدراكات والعلوم وأوسع الشهودات والفهوم إليه واعترافه بالعجز والقصور والحيرة فيه

**English:** The second way is for the most perfect apprehensions and sciences, and the widest witnessings and understandings, to come to their limit before Him and confess their incapacity, limitation and bewilderment concerning Him.

**Editorial decision:** Do not construe 'confession of incapacity' as ignorance of every divine name.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-031 · retain

**Arabic:** وبأن ما وراء غاية مدركه ونهاية مشهده بحار يحار في تيّارها فهم كل غائص واقف ، ويتيه في تيه كنهها وغور أسرارها علم كل خائض عارف

**English:** They acknowledge that beyond the furthest object of apprehension and the last horizon of witnessing are seas whose currents bewilder every diver's understanding, and deserts whose hidden depths confound the knowledge of every knowing traveler.

**Editorial decision:** Preserves the two images, seas and trackless wilderness.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-032 · retain

**Arabic:** هي المقصد الأعلى والمطلب الأولى ؛ كما نصّ صلّى اللّه تعالى عليه وسلم بقوله في مناجاته : « لا أحصى ثناء عليك »

**English:** These are the highest end and most worthy object of seeking; the Prophet indicated this in his supplication, 'I cannot enumerate the praise due to You.'

**Editorial decision:** The poetic plural 'these' refers back to inaccessible depths, not a positive list of predicates.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-033 · annotate

**Arabic:** لا أبلغ كل ما فيك ، وبقوله أيضا في أثناء دعائه : « أو استأثرت به في مكنون الغيب عندك »

**English:** That is, 'I cannot reach all that is in You'; and likewise in his supplication, 'or [a name] that You have reserved in the hidden mystery with You'.

**Editorial decision:** 'I cannot enumerate Your praise' appears in Ṣaḥīḥ Muslim 486; distinguish Farghānī's gloss from the hadith's words. The reserved names phrase comes from another supplication.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-034 · annotate

**Arabic:** وبقوله : « ربّ زدني فيك تحيّرا » ، وأمثال ذلك

**English:** And in the saying 'My Lord, increase me in bewilderment concerning You', and similar reports.

**Editorial decision:** Modern editor of B2007 p20 explicitly says he did not find this report in his sources; do not imply an established hadith.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-035 · retain

**Arabic:** ومهما علم أو شوهد أو أدرك شيء منها عند تجلّيها الظاهري أو الباطني أو الجمعي

**English:** Whatever of these realities is known, witnessed or apprehended through their outward, inward or comprehensive self-disclosure—

**Editorial decision:** The clause is conditional and the construction continues over the printed page boundary.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-036 · retain

**Arabic:** في السير الحبي وقرب النوافل ، وتقدم السلوك على الجذبة وسبق الفناء على البقاء

**English:** —in the journey of love and nearness through supererogatory works, where disciplined journeying precedes attraction and annihilation precedes subsistence—

**Editorial decision:** Keep distinctions: ḥubbī versus maḥbūbī and nawāfil versus farāʾiḍ, developed on p21.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-001-p18-20.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR001-037 · revise

**Arabic:** حيث يكون ويظهر لدى الفتح أن الحقّ المتجلّي آلة لإدراك العبد

**English:** —then at the spiritual opening it becomes apparent that the self-disclosing Real is the instrument by which the servant apprehends—

**Editorial decision:** The prior English 'may reveal' weakened the affirmative where Arabic says yakūn wa-yaẓhar. The sentence finishes on B2007 p21.

**English:** —the one to whom disclosure occurs, in the sense of 'I become his hearing and sight, so by Me he hears and sees'.

**Editorial decision:** Continuation of B2007 p20; distinguish report from authorial conceptual explanation.

**English:** In the journey of the beloved and the nearness of obligatory acts, where traveling follows attraction and original subsistence precedes annihilation—

**Editorial decision:** A reversal of the order assigned to the ḥubbī journey at p20.

**English:** —it becomes clear that the servant who receives disclosure is the instrument of the self-disclosing Real's apprehension, in the sense of 'God speaks on His servant's tongue: God hears the one who praises Him.'

**Editorial decision:** Instrument-to-apprehender relation is reversed relative to the earlier sentence.

### CR002-004 · retain

**Arabic:** وعند انتهاء السيرين والجمع بين الحكمين ابتداء وانتهاء حيث يصح أن كل واحد منهما مدركا وآلة على التعاقب أو معا في حالة واحدة من باب وَما رَمَيْتَ إِذْ رَمَيْتَ وَلكِنَّ اللَّهَ رَمى

**English:** At the completion of both journeys and their union in beginning and end, each can be the apprehender and the instrument, successively or together in one state, as in 'You did not throw when you threw, but God threw' (Qurʾān 8:17).

**Editorial decision:** The subject is the reciprocal grammatical distribution of apprehender and instrument, not equality of independent beings.

### CR002-005 · corrected

**Arabic:** فعلى كل حال يكون ذلك العلم والشهود والتجلّي والإدراك من حيث تعينه ومشيئته وعلمه المقدّس بذاته الأقدس تعالى وتقدّس من حيث واحديتها ، لا من حيث إطلاقها وأحديتها

**English:** In every case, this knowing, witnessing, disclosure and apprehension concern His determination, His will and His sacred knowledge of His most holy Essence—exalted and sanctified is He—under the consideration of its oneness (wāḥidiyya), not under that of its nondelimitation and unicity (aḥadiyya).

**Editorial decision:** The older F007 excerpt omitted 'من حيث واحديتها'. The corrected B2007 source is confirmed; the phrase also appears in a later quotation in al-Fanārī's Miṣbāḥ al-uns (modern printed text p183), though this is reception testimony not an independent manuscript witness. Direct historical-print check: I1876 p7 (PDF scan 11) also contains من حيث واحديتها. This confirms the correction of the earlier project excerpt; it is not a manuscript variant.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: true; medieval manuscript verified: false.

**Bounded historical printing comparison:** I1876 printed p7, scan 11: **من حيث واحديتها** (agreement); I1876 p7 confirms the phrase B2007 has, previously omitted in a GNAMMA excerpt.. This is not a manuscript variant.

### CR002-006 · retain

**Arabic:** فإن إدراكه وشهوده من حيث تعيّنه وعلمه غير ممنوع ، بل موكول إلى مشيئته تعالى وتقدّس كما قال عزّ من قائل : وَلا يُحِيطُونَ بِشَيْءٍ مِنْ عِلْمِهِ إِلَّا بِما شاءَ

**English:** For apprehending and witnessing Him under the consideration of His determination and knowledge is not prohibited; it is dependent upon His will, as He says, 'They encompass none of His knowledge except what He wills' (Qurʾān 2:255).

**Editorial decision:** The scope qualifier is essential to avoid treating the Essence's undelimitation as an object of determined knowledge.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-007 · retain

**Arabic:** ثم إن وحدته التي انتشئت منها الأحديّة والواحدية هي عين ذاته لا صفة زائدة ولا نعت زائد عليها

**English:** His unity, from which unicity and oneness derive, is identical with His Essence: it is neither an additional attribute nor an additional qualification.

**Editorial decision:** Do not identify two considerations with two distinct divine substances.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-008 · retain

**Arabic:** لكن للوحدة اعتباران أصليّان أوليّان ، أحدهما : سقوط الاعتبارات عنها بالكلية ، وسمّي الذات أحدا بهذا الاعتبار ومتعلقة بطون الذات وإطلاقها وأزليّتها

**English:** But unity admits two original, primary considerations. The first is the complete dropping away of considerations, under which the Essence is called One in unicity (aḥad), related to its hiddenness, nondelimitation and pre-eternity.

**Editorial decision:** Important to separate first determination (comprehensive intermediary) from aḥadiyya (one consideration).

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-009 · retain

**Arabic:** وعلى هذا يكون نسبة الاسم الأحد إلى السلب أحقّ من نسبته إلى الثبوت والإيجاب

**English:** Thus the name aḥad is more properly related to negation than to establishment and affirmation.

**Editorial decision:** Metaphysical status of the negative consideration.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-010 · retain

**Arabic:** والاعتبار الثاني : ثبوت الاعتبارات الغير المتناهية لها مع اندراجها فيها في أول رتبة الذات ، وتحقّق أكثر تفصيل تعيّناتها في ثاني الرتبة الآتي بيان كليهما عن قريب إن شاء اللّه تعالى

**English:** The second consideration is the establishment of infinitely many considerations for unity, included within it at the first rank of the Essence, while most of their determinate differentiation is realized at the second rank, as both will shortly be explained.

**Editorial decision:** Do not read second-rank explicit differentiation back into the first-rank inclusion.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-011 · retain

**English:** Like halfness, thirdness and quarterness, established and contained in the one considered here, from which numbers arise in existential ranks.

**Editorial decision:** Numerical analogy illustrates included relations, not arithmetic divisions within the Essence.

### CR002-012 · retain

**English:** And from these considerations arise the distinct two, three and four: the one is half of two, a third of three, a quarter of four, and so on.

**Editorial decision:** Keep ʿayn as determinate identity here, not the same sense as externally existing individual.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-013 · retain

**Arabic:** والذات بهذا الاعتبار يسمّى واحدا اسما ثبوتيّا لا سلبيّا ، ولم تصح إضافة هذه الاعتبارات المذكورة إلى الاسم الأحد لا حقيقة ولا وضعا لغويّا

**English:** Under this consideration the Essence is called wāḥid, an affirmative rather than a negative name. These considerations are not properly attributed to the name aḥad, either in reality or by linguistic convention.

**Editorial decision:** The last phrase continues in the next printed page with 'فحقائق هذه الاعتبارات'.

Page/source: https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/9606/22?lang=ar

### CR002-014 · retain

**Arabic:** الاعتبارات في أول رتبة الذات ثابتة للاسم الواحد الحقيقي ، ومندرجة فيه ثبوتا حقيقيّا واندراجا أصليّا

**English:** These considerations at the first rank of the Essence are established for the truly One and included within it by real establishment and original inclusion.

**Editorial decision:** Source presents true thubūt but does not equate it with external existential actuality.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-015 · retain

**Arabic:** كما أن أمثلتها وصورها ثابتة لهذا الواحد المشهود أثره لنا ههنا ، ومندرجة فيه غير قادحة في الرتبتين في صرافة هذا الواحد ، ولا منافية لها

**English:** As their analogues and forms are established within the one whose effect is witnessed here, their inclusion at either rank neither impairs nor contradicts the simplicity of that one.

**Editorial decision:** The clause explicitly controls the number analogy's ontological limits.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-016 · retain

**Arabic:** ومتعلق هذا الاعتبار الواحدي ظهور الذات ووجودها وأبديّتها ؛ كما أن متعلق الاعتبار الأحدي بطونها وأزليّتها

**English:** The consideration of oneness concerns the Essence's manifestation, existence and perpetuity; the consideration of unicity concerns its hiddenness and pre-eternity.

**Editorial decision:** Preserve the parallel between ẓuhūr / buṭūn and abad / azal.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-017 · retain

**Arabic:** ولا مغايرة ولا غيرية بين هذين الاعتبارين ، ولا بين كل اعتبار واعتبار من اعتبارات الواحدية في أول رتبة الذات الآتي بيانه

**English:** There is neither differentiation nor otherness between these two considerations, nor between one consideration of oneness and another at the first rank of the Essence.

**Editorial decision:** Distinguish mughāyara and ghayriyya as both negated, not just simple sameness.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-018 · retain

**Arabic:** لأن المغايرة والغيرية من أحكام الكثرة والمنافاة الثابتة بينها وبين الوحدة ، ولا كثرة ثمة تنافي الوحدة ، بل تلك الوحدة هي المنشئة لكل وحدة وكثرة مفهومة ههنا متغايرة عندنا

**English:** For difference and otherness are judgments of multiplicity and its opposition to unity. There, no multiplicity exists that opposes unity; that unity is the source of every unity and multiplicity understood here as different.

**Editorial decision:** This does not by itself negate real plurality in subsequent manifested ranks.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-019 · retain

**Arabic:** ولا يفهم ارتفاع هذه المغايرة في تلك الحضرة إلّا من ارتفع حالا ومقاما عن التقيّد بالمراتب المقيّدة بأحكام الكثرة والمقيّدة لأربابها بها

**English:** None understands the removal of such difference in that Presence unless he has risen, in state and station, beyond restriction by ranks determined by judgments of multiplicity and restricting their occupants through them.

**Editorial decision:** Explicit link between ontological rank and transformed apprehension.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-020 · retain

**Arabic:** ولارتفاع حكم هذه المغايرة في الرتبة الأولى حكم بها بعض أكابر المحقّقين من أهل اللّه بأن الواحد الأحد اسم واحد مركّب ؛ كبعلبك ومعديكرب

**English:** Because the judgment of difference is absent at the first rank, some eminent realized masters hold that al-wāḥid al-aḥad is one compound name, like Baʿlabakk or Maʿdīkarib.

**Editorial decision:** Grammatical examples are genuine proper-name compounds; not a third ontological rank.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-021 · retain

**Arabic:** ثم اعلم أن هذه الاعتبارات الثابتة للواحد ثمة ، والمندرجة فيه في أول رتبة الذات المحكوم عليها فيها بأنها عين الذات

**English:** Know, further, that these considerations established for the One and included within it at the first rank of the Essence are judged there to be the Essence itself.

**Editorial decision:** Differentiation at later ranks must not be inserted into this first-rank claim.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-022 · retain

**Arabic:** بحيث إن كل ما يضاف إلى الذات يضاف إلى كلّ واحد منها من الجمعية والاشتمال على الكلّ في هذه الرتبة الأولى بحسبها

**English:** Thus what is attributed to the Essence under the aspect of comprehensiveness and containing the whole is likewise attributed to each of the considerations at this rank.

**Editorial decision:** The qualifier 'at this rank' is essential.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-023 · retain

**Arabic:** نظرا من حيث مظاهرها وصورها وآثارها إليها ألفينا بعضها كلّيات وأصولا أول بمثابة أجناس عالية

**English:** If considered through their manifestations, forms and effects, some prove to be universals and primary principles comparable to supreme genera.

**Editorial decision:** Move from first-rank inclusion to what follows upon later manifestation.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-024 · retain

**Arabic:** هي مسمّى ألفاظ دالّة على حقائق أسماء الذات ومفهوماتها التي منها مفاتح الغيب ، والواحد الأحد وباطن اسم اللّه ، وباطن اسم الرحمن الرحيم ، ومفهوم جمع أسماء الضمائر

**English:** These are the realities named by words designating the essential names and their concepts: the Keys of the Unseen, the One-in-Unicity, the inward aspect of Allāh, the inward aspect of al-Raḥmān and al-Raḥīm, and the comprehensive meaning of pronominal names.

**Editorial decision:** Distinguish name-realities from their linguistic words, elaborated at later source pages.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-025 · retain

**Arabic:** ووجدنا بعض تلك الاعتبارات المندرجة في الواحدية من حيث النظر في مظاهرها وآثارها بمثابة أجناس تالية وأنواع مندرجة فيها وهلمّ جرّا إلى أشخاص هاوية إلى الدّركات الجزئية جدّا

**English:** Some of the considerations included within oneness, considered through their effects, are like lower genera, species included within them, and so on down to individuals at the most narrowly particular levels.

**Editorial decision:** Avoid treating genera and species here as independent eternally existing substances.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-026 · retain

**Arabic:** التي بها يحكم على الحضرة الأبدية بعدم التناهي كثرة وتعيّنات وتقيّدات ، والكل ثابتة بصورها المعنوية في الرتبة الثانية بعضها بطريق التشخّص ، وبعضها بطريق الاندراج في الأصناف والأنواع

**English:** These yield the judgment that the perpetual Presence contains endlessly many determinations and restrictions. All are established as intelligible forms at the second rank, some individually, others by inclusion within kinds and species.

**Editorial decision:** Explicit second-rank establishment versus first-rank inclusion.

### CR002-027 · retain

**Arabic:** نعم ، وقد تعيّنت وانضبطت جملة منها في اللّوح المحفوظ ، وتصوّرت صورا وجودية روحانية فيه منتهيا ظهورها بانتهاء يوم القيامة مجملا ومفصّلا

**English:** A group of these is also determined and fixed in the Preserved Tablet, acquiring spiritual existential forms whose display extends to the Resurrection, both comprehensively and in detail.

**Editorial decision:** Not a blanket claim that all eternal knowables manifest in this way.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-028 · retain

**Arabic:** ويتفصّل منه في المراتب الوجودية مجملا في العرش ومفصّلا في الكرسي دفعة واحدة ، ثم يتفصّل منهما في الأركان والسماوات على التعاقب إلى أنهى مراتب

**English:** From the Tablet it is unfolded within existential ranks, comprehensively in the Throne and in detail in the Footstool in one act, and thereafter successively in the elements and heavens down to the lowest ranks—

### CR002-029 · retain

**Arabic:** الكون مثالا وحسّا ، وفي المواطن دنيا وبرزخا وآخرة ، وينتهي إلى ههنا جميع الشهودات والإدراكات والمكاشفات الحاصلة للكمّل والأولياء

**English:** —of the cosmos, in imaginal and sensory form and in its worldly, intermediate and hereafter abodes. All witnessing, apprehension and unveiling attained by perfected people and saints end at this limit.

**Editorial decision:** Continues p22 sentence; note distinct metaphysical modes and abodes.

**English:** The lowest among them and the highest are alike with respect to this limit.

**Editorial decision:** The sentence denies no distinction in actual spiritual rank; only the epistemic horizon.

### CR002-031 · corrected

**Arabic:** إلّا أنه ربما يكون في الحضرة الغيبية الأزليّة نحو هذه الكلّيات والجزئيّات أمور باطنة وأحوال مستورة لم تتعيّن بعد لا في الرتبة الثانية ولا في اللّوح المحفوظ

**English:** Yet in the eternal unseen Presence there may perhaps be, regarding these universals and particulars, hidden things and concealed states not yet determined either at the second rank or in the Preserved Tablet.

**Editorial decision:** PHOTOGRAPHIC PRINT-TO-PRINT DIFFERENCE I1876 printed p8/scan12: سوى أنه ربما يكون; B2007 printed p23: إلّا أنه ربما يكون. Prefer I1876 connective provisionally; the modal ربما and the uncertain status of undetermined affairs must remain. Medieval witnesses not yet collated.

**Bounded historical printing comparison:** I1876 printed p8, scan 12: **سوى أنه ربما يكون** (historical-print-difference); Original-resolution historical I1876 p8 has سوى أنه, while B2007 p23 reads إلا أنه. Both are exceptive; adopt earlier print provisionally, with the Beirut reading preserved, without changing the modal nuance of ربما. No manuscript compared.. This is not a manuscript variant.

### CR002-032 · crux

**Arabic:** فلم تعلم ولم يحكم بشيء منها إلّا بعد تعيّنها ووقوعها في المراتب الوجودية بالتدريج مع الآنات

**English:** Accordingly they are not known or judged until their determination and occurrence in existential ranks, progressively with successive instants.

**Editorial decision:** Historical print agreement now established at I1876 pp8–9 (scans12–13): the source really has فلم تعلم ولم يحكم بشيء منها إلا بعد تعينها. Preserve the claim exactly; it remains a difficult question about determinacy and knowledge, not evidence for an invented manuscript emendation.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: true; medieval manuscript verified: false.

**Bounded historical printing comparison:** I1876 printed p8–9, scan 12–13: **فلم تعلم ولم يحكم بشيء منها إلا بعد تعينها** (agreement); Historic 1876 printing p8 end and p9 beginning preserve the difficult expression. This is a bounded print agreement; theological interpretation and manuscript text remain unresolved.. This is not a manuscript variant.

### CR002-033 · retain

**Arabic:** وهي أبطن بطون أحكام الغيب الذاتي المغلوب والمسبوق بالرحمة الذاتية

**English:** These belong to the utmost inward depth of the judgments of the essential Unseen, which is preceded and overcome by essential mercy.

**Editorial decision:** Distinguish hiddenness of rank from an event changing the Essence.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-034 · annotate

**Arabic:** فلهذا أوجبت الرهبة والخشية المشار إليها في قوله تعالى : قُلْ ما كُنْتُ بِدْعاً مِنَ الرُّسُلِ وَما أَدْرِي ما يُفْعَلُ بِي وَلا بِكُمْ

**English:** They therefore occasion awe and fear, as indicated by 'Say: I am no novelty among messengers, and I do not know what will be done with me or with you' (Qurʾān 46:9).

**Editorial decision:** Farghānī's interpretation of the verse is an exegetical move; the verse alone does not prove his exact metaphysical thesis.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-035 · annotate

**Arabic:** ومن وجه أيضا يشير إليها قوله صلّى اللّه عليه وسلّم شفقا منها : « ليت ربّ محمد لم يخلق محمدا »

**English:** And from another aspect the saying attributed to the Prophet, expressing concern, 'Would that Muḥammad's Lord had not created Muḥammad', is taken as an allusion to them.

**Editorial decision:** The Beirut editor explicitly reports no source found for this statement. Do not attribute it as authenticated hadith.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-036 · retain

**Arabic:** مع أنه كان من حيث ما تعيّن من حاله صلّى اللّه عليه وسلّم في الحضرة العلمية واللّوح المحفوظ على بصيرة من ربّه

**English:** Yet concerning what had become determined of the Prophet's state in the Presence of Knowledge and the Preserved Tablet, he possessed insight from his Lord.

**Editorial decision:** Distinguish this determined register from the unqualified Unseen of preceding clauses.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-037 · annotate

**Arabic:** حتى أنه كان يقول اعتمادا عليها ونظرا إليها : « آدم ومن دونه تحت لوائي يوم القيامة ولا فخر » ، « ولو كان موسى حيّا لما وسعه إلّا اتّباعي »

**English:** Thus in reliance on it he could say, 'Adam and those below him are under my banner on the Day of Resurrection, without boasting', and 'Were Moses alive, he would have no option but to follow me'.

**Editorial decision:** Modern editor lists differing hadith collections for reports; do not treat every cited report as identically authenticated.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-038 · retain

**Arabic:** وقوله : « أنا أول من يقرع باب الجنّة وأول من يفتح باب الشفاعة » ونحو ذلك من الإشارات في أحاديث صحاح

**English:** And 'I am the first to knock at the gate of Paradise and the first to open the gate of intercession', and comparable indications from reports he regards as sound.

**Editorial decision:** Convey the authorial assessment 'aḥādīth ṣiḥāḥ' without issuing an independent authentication judgment.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-039 · retain

**Arabic:** وعلى الحقيقة كثيرا ما يجرّه الدعاء والسؤال من الأمور والأحكام الغيبية إنما يكون من تلك الأمور والأحوال

**English:** In reality, much of what supplication and petition draw forth from unseen affairs and judgments pertains to those hidden things and states.

**Editorial decision:** The relationship of supplication to hidden affairs needs comparison with his account of fixed divine knowledge.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-040 · retain

**Arabic:** فإن ما عداها لا يتحصّل إلّا المكتوب الثابت المقسوم في الحضرة العلمية لا يزيد ولا ينقص

**English:** Apart from these, nothing occurs except what is written, established and allotted in the Presence of Knowledge, without increase or decrease.

**Editorial decision:** Sets up tension with 'not known until they occur'; do not resolve by paraphrase.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-002-p21-23.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR002-041 · retain

**Arabic:** ثم إنه قد عبّر بعض العلماء باللّه الواقفين على حضراته الكلية عن بعض اعتبارات الواحدية المندرجة فيها في الرتبة الأولى بالشؤون الذاتية

**English:** Some knowers of God, acquainted with His universal Presences, have called certain considerations of oneness, included in it at the first rank, 'essential affairs' (shuʾūn dhātiyya).

**English:** These appear at the second and lower ranks as principal realities and certain enduring concomitant attributes, such as life and knowledge.

**Editorial decision:** Thubūt of principal realities versus dependent qualities.

**English:** And as enduring beings such as individual entities and substances, and as properties of their continuance and stability, considered with respect to some—

### CR003-001 · revise

**Arabic:** معاني الشأن وهو الأمر

**English:** among the meanings of shaʾn, that is, 'affair' or 'command'.

**Editorial decision:** A grammatical continuation of p.23; do not create a new sentence at the printed page break. Shaʾn is not a temporal event of divine change.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-002 · retain

**Arabic:** وعبّر بعضهم عن بعضها بالأحوال الذاتية

**English:** Some have designated certain of these as essential states (aḥwāl dhātiyya).

**Editorial decision:** 'Some' is the author's attribution; he is reporting a usage, not necessarily establishing a universal technical definition.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-003 · annotate

**Arabic:** كالقبض والبسط ، والرضا والغضب ، وجميع الأعراض التي لا تدوم

**English:** such as contraction and expansion, satisfaction and anger, and all accidents that do not endure.

**Editorial decision:** The examples concern appearances and transformations in manifestation; do not infer accidental change in the divine Essence itself.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-004 · revise

**Arabic:** تذكر تحوّل الحق تعالى وتقدّس من صورة لم يعرفها قوم في الآخرة إلى صورة يعرفونه بها

**English:** Recall the Real's appearing, in the hereafter, in a form they do not recognize, then in a form in which they recognize Him.

**Editorial decision:** The source ties the example to a transmitted report and recognitional difference, not an unqualified doctrine of bodily change.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-005 · retain

**Arabic:** اعلم أن أوّل اعتبار وتعيّن تعيّن من الغيب هذه الوحدة التي انتشأت منها الأحدية والواحدية

**English:** Know that the first consideration and determination issuing from the Unseen is the unity from which aḥadiyya and wāḥidiyya arise.

**Editorial decision:** Do not identify first determination as already the differentiated second rank; aḥadiyya and wāḥidiyya are two respects of unity.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-006 · retain

**Arabic:** فظلّت برزخا جامعا بينهما

**English:** It remains an intermediary (barzakh) encompassing both.

**Editorial decision:** Barzakh here denotes a comprehensive mediating respect, not a third material realm inserted between two divine substances.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-007 · crux

**Arabic:** كحقيقة المحبة المنتشئة منها المحبّية والمحبوبية وكونها جامعة بينهما ورافعة بينهما موحّدة إياهما

**English:** like the reality of love, from which loving and being beloved arise; it encompasses both, removes what divides them, and unites them.

**Editorial decision:** The printed wording ورافعة بينهما is compressed: a missing/elliptical object after rāfiʿa may underlie 'removes what divides them.' Maintain provisional reading; no manuscript emendation.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-008 · retain

**Arabic:** هذا الاعتبار والتعيّن الأول الذي هو عين الذات بحكم البرزخية والجمعية المذكورة

**English:** this first consideration and determination, which is identical with the Essence under the stated consideration of intermediacy and comprehensiveness.

**Editorial decision:** Identity is predicated 'under the consideration of' (bi-ḥukm), not a claim of a second additional divine entity.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-009 · revise

**Arabic:** فكانت هذه القابلية أصل كل قابلية وفاعلية أيضا

**English:** This receptivity is the root of every receptivity and also of all activity.

**Editorial decision:** Qābiliyya and fāʿiliyya are correlated active/receptive principial considerations, not created passivity and action.

### CR003-010 · revise

**Arabic:** والأسمائي المتعلّق بذلك الظهور حديثا وإخبارا نزيها بحرف نزيه وحداني هو عين الذات

**English:** and name-related perfection concerning that manifestation: an immaterial discourse and informing through a single transcendent 'letter' identical with the Essence.

**Editorial decision:** Sentence continues p.24. 'Letter' is expressly non-corporeal; not a phonetic sound or a separate divine component.

### CR003-011 · retain

**Arabic:** متضمّن جميع المعاني كلّياتها وجزئياتها

**English:** containing all meanings, universal and particular.

**Editorial decision:** Meaningful totality is predicated of the inward unitary letter before differentiation at articulated levels.

### CR003-012 · retain

**Arabic:** في سابع رتب أبطن الكلام

**English:** at the seventh rank of speech's inward depths.

**Editorial decision:** The ordinal counts inward from the external, articulated expression; do not conflate the seven 'inward depths' with seven historical stages.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-013 · annotate

**Arabic:** إن للقرآن ظهرا وبطنّا ، وفي بطنه بطن إلى سبعة أبطن

**English:** The Qurʾān has an outward and an inward, and within its inward a further inward, to seven inward depths.

**Editorial decision:** Reported saying appears in Farghānī's authorial text, but the modern editor states this exact wording was not located. Do not mark as authenticated prophetic ḥadīth.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-014 · retain

**Arabic:** كما يتحدّث أحدنا في نفسه بلا واسطة حرف وصوت ظاهر

**English:** as one of us speaks inwardly without an outward letter or audible sound.

**Editorial decision:** Farghānī explicitly says the comparison is analogical and excludes physical utterance.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-015 · revise

**Arabic:** قابلية ميل الذات بالسماع والإصغاء إلى ذلك الحديث هي أثر تلك المحبّة في الحقيقة

**English:** the Essence's receptive inclination toward hearing and listening to this discourse is in truth the effect of that love.

**Editorial decision:** Inclination, love, and hearing here name principial relational considerations, not change caused by lack in the Essence.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-016 · retain

**Arabic:** ملاحظة الذات نور جمالها الذي هو عين واحديتها وظهورها وظهور اعتباراتها

**English:** the Essence's beholding the light of its beauty, which is identical with its oneness, manifestation, and manifestation of its considerations.

**Editorial decision:** Keep the repeated 'manifestation' rather than flattening the subject–object relation.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-017 · revise

**Arabic:** ليميط خمار غلبة حكم جلال الغيب والبطون عن الجمال والظهور

**English:** so as to remove the veil of the preponderance of the majesty of the Unseen and hiddenness from beauty and manifestation.

**Editorial decision:** The digital source writes ليميط without vocalization; the 'veil' is figurative and must not be literalized.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-018 · crux

**Arabic:** بتغليب الرحمة وسبقها على أنها غايات حكم الغضب عارجا لا نازلا

**English:** through the preponderance and precedence of mercy, in contrast to the furthest limit of the judgment called anger, upward rather than downward.

**Editorial decision:** I1876 p10 / scan14 now confirms the bounded concluding phrase حكم الغضب عارجا لا نازلا. This historic PRINT agreement does not decide the syntactic attachment of على أنها غايات and does not justify smoothing the whole sentence.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: true; medieval manuscript verified: false.

**Bounded historical printing comparison:** I1876 printed p10, scan 14: **حكم الغضب عارجا لا نازلا** (agreement); Narrow ending contrast is photographed in I1876 p10/scan14 and B2007 p25. Earlier على أنها غايات remains syntactically unsettled.. This is not a manuscript variant.

### CR003-019 · retain

**Arabic:** فإن الظهور واللاظهور كان في حكم اقتضاء هذه القابلية الأولية على السواء

**English:** For manifestation and non-manifestation stood on an equal footing with respect to what this primordial receptivity entails.

**Editorial decision:** This is a logical/principial contrast, not a chronological episode when God lacked manifestation.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-020 · retain

**Arabic:** فوجدت الذات نفسها بهذا التجلّي الذي هو عين العلم والشهود بحكم مرآته المذكورة

**English:** The Essence found itself through this self-disclosure, which itself is knowledge and witnessing, under the consideration of the mirror already mentioned.

**Editorial decision:** 'Mirror' is identical with Essence under a respect, not a second existing object.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-021 · annotate

**Arabic:** فإن القول عبارة عن نفس منبعث من باطن المتنفّس

**English:** Speech is a breath issuing from the inward aspect of the one breathing.

**Editorial decision:** Analogical lexical analysis of 'speech' and 'breath': do not transfer corporeal exhalation to the Essence.

### CR003-022 · retain

**Arabic:** والسمع هو قبول ذلك المعنى ظاهرا أو باطنا

### CR003-024 · retain

**Arabic:** والقوة تمكن مما يطلب ظهوره

### CR003-025 · crux

**Arabic:** فلفظ وكلي بي لسان محدث

**English:** My utterance—and my whole being is a tongue speaking through me.

Collateral poetic reading: B2007 p26 gives ولحظ ... عين لعبرة, whereas a separate published Tāʾiyya in the Qayṣarī commentary (verse 545) and Wikisource read لعبرتي. These are parallel poem texts, NOT independent Farghānī manuscript witnesses.

### CR003-026 · revise

**Arabic:** معاني صفات ما ورى اللّبس أثبتت

**English:** The meanings of the attributes were established beyond their clothing in multiplicity.

**Editorial decision:** Lubs ('putting on' or 'clothing') is the metaphor for differentiation of what is not yet distinct at the first rank; previous 'beyond the clothing of appearance' under-specified this.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-027 · retain

**English:** attributes distinct from one another at the second rank according to that rank's law.

**Editorial decision:** Explicit differentiation occurs at second rank; avoid transferring independent multiplicity to the Essence at first rank.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-028 · retain

**English:** though at that first rank they were not attributes but considerations neither distinct nor mutually divergent.

**Editorial decision:** Critical distinction: shūʾūn/considerations at first rank vs distinct attributes at second rank.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-029 · retain

**Arabic:** وباطنها الأول : أن تكون هذه الصفات مضافة إلى نفسه

**English:** The first inward level is the attribution of these qualities to the individual's soul.

**Editorial decision:** The 'first inward' degree follows their outward manifestation in bodily faculties, and so is not the initial divine self-disclosure.

### CR003-030 · revise

### CR003-031 · retain

**Arabic:** وباطنها الثاني : ما يضاف إلى نفسه من حيث عقله المنوّر بنور الشرع والإيمان باللّه

**English:** The second inward level pertains to the soul insofar as its intellect is illuminated by revealed law and faith in God.

**Editorial decision:** Law and faith illuminate intellect, enabling reorientation from world to afterlife; do not collapse into generic philosophical insight.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-032 · retain

**Arabic:** وباطنها الثالث : ما يضاف منها إلى روحه الروحانية المجرّدة

**English:** The third inward level pertains to the individual's immaterial spiritual spirit.

**Editorial decision:** Preserve the doubled expression rūḥuhu al-rūḥāniyya; it differentiates this level from ordinary psychical faculty.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-033 · retain

**Arabic:** الثابت تعيّنها وتميّزها في عالم الأرواح واللّوح المحفوظ

**English:** whose determination and distinctness are established in the world of spirits and the Preserved Tablet.

**Editorial decision:** That distinctness belongs to a cosmic level; no reason to infer independent divine existence.

### CR003-034 · revise

**Arabic:** وباطنها الرابع : ما يضاف منها إلى الوجود العيني المضاف إلى حقيقة الإنسانية

**English:** The fourth inward level concerns concrete existence as related to the reality of humanity.

**Editorial decision:** ʿAynī is actual/concrete rather than 'eye-like'; at this level the same senses are interpreted as disclosures of existential reality.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-035 · retain

**Arabic:** المقيّد بها والمظهر لأحكامها في مراتب الكون روحا ومثالا وحسّا

**English:** delimited by it and manifesting its rulings in cosmic ranks as spirit, image, and sense.

**Editorial decision:** The three modalities remain distinguishable even when a single principle is at issue.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-036 · revise

**Arabic:** وباطنها الخامس : ما يضاف منها إلى قلبه القابل التجلّي الوجود الباطني فيه

**English:** The fifth inward level relates to the heart receptive to the disclosure of inward existence within it.

**Editorial decision:** This is the heart's capacity of reception, not a claim of spatial containment of God.

### CR003-037 · annotate

**Arabic:** وسعني قلب عبدي

**English:** The heart of My servant encompassed Me.

**Editorial decision:** Farghānī cites a well-known spiritual saying; its role in his argument does not authenticate it as a prophetic or divine ḥadīth.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-038 · retain

**Arabic:** وباطنها السادس : ما يضاف منها إلى الوجود المطلق الرحمني الجمعي

**English:** The sixth inward level relates to comprehensive, merciful, nondelimited existence.

**Editorial decision:** 'Nondelimited' here belongs to the specific sixth-level perspective, not automatically identical with absolutely unconditioned Essence.

**English:** Some have the third gate opened: they are among the elect of the believers.

**Editorial decision:** The ascent concerns differential openings of inward capacities, not simply social class.

**Editorial decision:** Keep iḥsān as technical spiritual term.

**English:** for the seventh inward depth belongs particularly to him—God bless him and grant him peace.

**Editorial decision:** A Muhammadan inheritor's partial opening differs from the station's proper privilege; do not construe the sentences as a flat contradiction.

**English:** insofar as it is contemplation, it includes awareness of that perfection's diffusion through the detailed considerations of oneness.

**Editorial decision:** Here knowledge is indexed to wāḥidiyya and detailed considerations, not bare aḥadiyya.

**English:** insofar as it involves inclining in hearing and attentive listening, it entails a consideration underlying will.

**Editorial decision:** Do not infer psychological appetite from the term 'inclination'.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-003-p24-29.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR003-049 · revise

**Arabic:** وحكم تحقيق تفصيل الكمال ، وتحصيل شرائطه متضمّن حقيقة الجود بإفاضة الوجود على حقيقة كل موجود

**English:** The realization of perfection in detail, with its conditions, entails the reality of generosity through the bestowal of existence on the reality of each existent.

**English:** that is, the two bows of unity and multiplicity, or receptivity and activity, or necessity and possibility.

**English:** and the consideration of knowledge, whose reality is the appearance of the very reality, so that its effect is present in the one to whom it appears.

**English:** light as that which discloses the concealed, and witnessing as presence with the witnessed.

**English:** In the ranks of the cosmos only creation appears; in the ranks of the Real only the Real appears.

**Editorial decision:** Preserve the two predicative standpoints; avoid denying divine causal action in the cosmos.

**English:** None knows, witnesses, or sees the Real except the Real Himself or through Him.

**Editorial decision:** Alternative 'through Him' is in the Arabic and indispensable for the experiential register.

### CR004-013 · retain

**Arabic:** فكان متعلّقا بمعلوم واحد

### CR004-014 · annotate

**English:** Its expression, at this first rank, is transitive to one object.

**Editorial decision:** Farghānī makes an unusual verbal-transitivity analogy: it is his own grammatical illustration, not a general rule about the verb ʿalima; explicate with caution.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-004-p30-36.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

**English:** Knowledge at the completion of the second rank has real multiplicity and a collective relative unity in respect of its known contents.

**Editorial decision:** Do not ascribe multiplicity to the undelimited Essence without the second-rank qualifier.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-004-p30-36.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

**Editorial decision:** Refers to determination qua its intrinsic reality, not every limited predicate.

**English:** He was in ʿamāʾ; the following phrase concerning air above and below remains syntactically disputed.

**Editorial decision:** The Arabic phrase has competing interpretations: mā as negation (no air above/below) versus relative/interrogative or a variant without mā. **English:** The second class belongs to the cosmos: poverty, intrinsic nonbeing, lowliness and possibility.

### CR005-023 · revise

**English:** The third is attributed primarily to the Real and secondarily to the cosmos by attribution of existence to it.

**Editorial decision:** The same label (such as knowledge) can be predicated in differing modes; do not equate divine and creaturely possession.

## Beirut B2007 printed page 43 · B2007 modern digitized edited source

### CR005-024 · retain

**Arabic:** فأمّا الكلّيات والمتبوعات ، فما برحت محصورة في مبدأ الحضرة العلمية والمرتبة الثانية

**English:** Universals and principal realities never depart from the source of the Presence of Knowledge, the second rank.

**Editorial decision:** Principal intelligibles remain at their source; their concomitants manifest through spiritual, imaginal and sensible ranks.

### CR005-025 · annotate

**English:** The universal ranks number five—two attributed to the Real and three to the cosmos—and a sixth that unites them.

**Editorial decision:** The count is FIVE plus a SIXTH comprehensive rank; do not collapse this into five equally homogeneous ranks.

**English:** In the language, creation has three senses, the first being determination of measure.

**Editorial decision:** The following two are combining and cutting. The three lexical senses explain changing name affiliations.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-006-p48-49-51-60.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR006-027 · annotate

**Arabic:** فهو من توابع اسم القادر والمريد والقائل والعالم والحيّ والجواد والمقسط على السواء

**English:** It follows alike upon the Powerful, Willing, Speaker, Knowing, Living, Generous and Equitable.

**Editorial decision:** The explicit enumeration of all seven basic names *includes القائل*, powerfully constraining p38 digital القابل; still not a manuscript variant.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-006-p48-49-51-60.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR006-028 · retain

**Arabic:** فالقدرة ظاهرة والقوة باطنها

**English:** Power is its outward aspect, while strength is its inward aspect.

**Editorial decision:** Farghānī distinguishes qudra at the actual onset of action from quwwa prior to commencement.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-006-p48-49-51-60.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

## Beirut B2007 printed page 57 · B2007 modern digitized edited source

Page/source: https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/9606/57?lang=ar

### CR006-029 · revise

**Arabic:** فالخالق يقدّر الصور كلّها ، والباري يصوّر الصور الروحانية والمثالية ، والمصوّر يصور الصور الحسّية

**English:** The Creator determines all forms; the Originator fashions spiritual and imaginal forms; the Fashioner forms sensible forms.

**Editorial decision:** Distinguish three creative aspects instead of rendering all as generic 'creation'. The text assigns non-sensory forms specifically to al-Bāriʾ.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-006-p48-49-51-60.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR006-030 · retain

**Arabic:** أعني على غير مثال خارج عن علمه تعالى

**English:** That is, without a model external to His knowledge.

**Editorial decision:** Explicitly qualifies 'without precedent': creative novelty is not outside the timeless Divine knowledge.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-006-p48-49-51-60.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR006-031 · retain

**Arabic:** فكانت هذه الأسماء من وجه من توابع العالم ، ومن وجه من توابع القادر

**English:** From one perspective these names are subordinate to the Knowing and from another to the Powerful.

**Editorial decision:** One name can have multiple conceptual affiliations without contradiction.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-006-p48-49-51-60.js` · `B2007` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

**Editorial decision:** Farghānī introduces this as a tentative figurative reading of the ḥadīth, not a binding literal doctrine or a proven sole interpretation.

**Arabic:** إضافة الأحكام والتأثيرات إلى الأسباب ظاهرة ، وإلى مسبّبها باطنة

**English:** Attribution of rulings and effects to causes is outwardly manifest, while attribution to their true Causer remains hidden.

**Editorial decision:** Distinguish proximate secondary causes in the world from disclosure of ultimate sovereignty; avoids overreading as denial of causal order.

**Arabic:** ومن توابع اسم الملك أيضا ( الاسم الحكم )

**Editorial decision:** The Judging name acts through right adjudication and restoring rightful holdings.

**Arabic:** فيتصرّف فيهم تصرف الملاك على ظواهرهم وبواطنهم ، لا تصرف الملوك على ظواهرهم دون بواطنهم

**English:** He governs them with ownership over their outward and inward realities, unlike kings who control the outward alone.

### CR006-038 · revise

**English:** He then hands them over to the name the Restorer, who returns creatures step by step to the source from which their descent began.

**Editorial decision:** One of several differentiated deaths under al-Mumīt: physical death, death of fitra through hardness, and discipline of the commanding soul.

### CR007-001 · retain

**Arabic:** فذلك الإيثار يكون بالوجود أولا ، ثم بالبقاء ثانيا ، ثم بنفخ الروح والحياة ثالثا

**English:** That bestowal occurs first as existence, second as continuance, and third as the breathing of spirit and life.

**Editorial decision:** Three fundamental gifts precede the particular modes of spiritual, psychic and bodily sustenance.

### CR007-002 · retain

**English:** Next as spiritual nourishment, such as guidance, faith and their degrees.

**Editorial decision:** Fourth in a sevenfold enumeration, not a separate eighth item.

### CR007-003 · annotate

**Arabic:** وبإظهار آثار هذه الأخلاق فيه والمعاملة معه بها خامسا

**English:** Fifth, by showing the effects of those noble traits in the servant and treating him in accordance with them.

**Editorial decision:** Distinct from granting the traits themselves under the fourth mode.

**English:** Sixth, through psychic or personal provisions such as standing, eminence and dignity in both lives.

**Editorial decision:** Nafsānī should not be conflated with the preceding rūḥānī.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-007-p61-69.js` · `D-Forum-B2007-derivative` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR007-005 · annotate

**English:** Its distinctive privilege is bestowing existence and excellence in the first origination, nothing else.

**Editorial decision:** Attribution of a distinct function to al-Jawād despite its branches participating in many further gifts.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-007-p61-69.js` · `D-Forum-B2007-derivative` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

**English:** The Noble encompasses, in its senses, everything included in the Generous.

**Editorial decision:** Sentence continues with crucial exceptions: original existence, original continuance and excellence in fashioning.

**Editorial decision:** Do not generalize every perfection of al-Jawād as transferable to al-Karīm.

**English:** With it He covers the form of deviation designated as sin.

**Editorial decision:** Continuation of the grammatical sentence crossing p61–62; do not detach subject of the covering.

### CR007-010 · revise

**English:** Hence this meaning occurs most often in intensive forms: al-Ghaffār indicating generality and al-Ghafūr multiplicity.

**Editorial decision:** Older English 'universality of repeated covering' for al-Ghaffār risks collapsing تعميما (generality) into تعديدا (multiplicity).

**English:** From the obedient He conceals His hidden enabling grace, attributing deeds and their recompense to them in kindness.

**Editorial decision:** A paradox of divine agency and human responsibility; preserve causal rather than antinomian framing.

**English:** He then conceals all these from His friends, so that they witness everything as His favor.

**Editorial decision:** Different modes of concealment for sinners, obedient servants and friends.

**Editorial decision:** A different aspect of mercy than the concealment under ghafūr.

**Data origin:** `data/critical-pass-007-p61-69.js` · `D-Forum-B2007-derivative` · historical-print verified: false; medieval manuscript verified: false.

### CR007-016 · retain

**Arabic:** التواب هو العوّاد إلى الرحمة

**English:** Al-Tawwāb is the One who repeatedly returns to mercy.

**Editorial decision:** Cited as the statement of Abū Madyan; keep attribution separate from Prophetic report.
